  ## Iteration 55 ÔÇö Tab Polish & Title Bar Depth
  
  **Goal**: Improve tab bar visual consistency and depth by adding shadows to
  all tabs, making close buttons discoverable on inactive tabs, aligning
  icon/label sizes, adding rest-state borders, and strengthening the title bar
  gradient.
  
  **Changes made**:
  
  1. **Tab depth consistency** (`tab_bar.rs`): Inactive tabs now have a subtle
     drop shadow (0.08 alpha, 0.5px offset, 2px blur) instead of no shadow at
     all. Active tabs keep their existing stronger shadow (0.22 alpha). This
     creates smoother depth transitions when switching tabs ÔÇö shadows no longer
     abruptly appear/disappear.
  
  2. **Tab icon/label size alignment** (`tab_bar.rs`): Process icon glyph size
     reduced from 14px to 13px to match the tab label text size. This fixes a
     subtle vertical alignment/visual weight imbalance between icon and text.
  
  3. **Inactive tab close button visibility** (`tab_bar.rs`): Close buttons on
     inactive tabs now render at 25% text_color opacity instead of fully
     transparent. This provides discoverability (users can see the ├ù exists)
     while keeping the button unobtrusive at rest. Active tabs and hover states
     remain unchanged.
  
  4. **Title bar gradient strength** (`title_bar.rs`): Gradient offset increased
     from 0.012 to 0.020 (closer to tab bar's 0.025). The title bar gradient
     was nearly invisible; now it has a subtle but perceptible depth cue that
     better matches the tab bar's gradient language.
  
  5. **Inactive tab rest-state borders** (`tab_bar.rs`): Inactive tabs now show
     a very faint border (BORDER_VARIANT ├ù 0.15 alpha) at rest, with width 1.0
     (was 0.0). This gives tabs visible shape definition without hover,
     improving scanability of the tab bar at a glance.
  
  **Design principle**: This iteration targets **visual continuity** ÔÇö ensuring
  that tab states transition smoothly (depth, borders, close buttons) rather
  than having binary on/off visual properties. Professional apps like VS Code
  and Zed maintain subtle visual presence for all interactive elements, even
  when inactive.
  
  **Result**: The tab bar reads as a cohesive strip of well-defined tab shapes
  with consistent depth, rather than a collection of invisible regions that
  only become visible on hover or activation. Close buttons are discoverable
  without hovering. The title bar has more perceptible depth gradient.
  
  **Visual comparison with reference**:
  - Tab depth consistency: Ô£ô All tabs have shadow presence, smooth transitions
  - Close button discoverability: Ô£ô Faint ├ù visible like VS Code/Zed
  - Icon-label alignment: Ô£ô Both 13px, no vertical weight mismatch
  - Title bar depth: Ô£ô Gradient now perceptible, matches tab bar language
  - Tab shape at rest: Ô£ô Faint borders provide shape without visual noise
  
  **Remaining gaps vs reference (iteration 55)**:
  - Terminal content not displaying (needs daemon running)
  - Multi-pane terminal layout needs daemon content
  
  ## Iteration 56 ÔÇö Visual Quieting Pass
  
  **Goal**: Reduce visual noise by flattening surfaces, softening borders, and
  compacting the breadcrumb bar. Match Zed's principle of letting color difference
  provide separation rather than visible border lines.
  
  **Changes made**:
  
  1. **Sidebar background flattened** (`ui/sidebar.rs`): Bottom darkening reduced
     from 10% to 4%. The sidebar is now nearly flat, matching Zed/VS Code's
     restrained surface treatment. Eliminates visible gradient banding.
  
  2. **Sidebar right border softened** (`ui/sidebar.rs`): Hairline alpha reduced
     from 0.18 to 0.12. Inward shadow shortened (4pxÔåÆ3px) and lightened
     (0.04ÔåÆ0.03 alpha). Inner shadow reduced (0.03ÔåÆ0.02 alpha, 4pxÔåÆ3px blur).
     The BG_DARKÔåÆBG_BASE color difference now provides primary separation.
  
  3. **Breadcrumb bar compacted** (`ui/layout.rs`): Height reduced from 22px to
     20px logical, gaining 2 logical pixels (3 physical at 1.5├ù) for the
     terminal content area. More compact like VS Code's breadcrumb.
  
  4. **Breadcrumb gradient softened** (`main.rs`): Top brightness reduced from
     88% to 92%, bottom from 96% to 98%. The gradient is now subtle enough
     to avoid visible banding in the compact 20px bar.
  
  5. **Breadcrumb bottom separator softened** (`main.rs`): Alpha reduced from
     0.30 to 0.20. The gradient difference between breadcrumb and content area
     provides primary separation.
  
  6. **Tab bar branding right border softened** (`ui/tab_bar.rs`): Alpha reduced
     from 0.18 to 0.12, matching the sidebar right border.
  
  7. **Status bar bottom accent stripe widened** (`ui/status_bar.rs`): Changed
     from 1px hline to 2px fill bar, matching the 2px top accent stripe for
     cohesive visual window framing.
  
  8. **Status bar top separator softened** (`ui/status_bar.rs`): Alpha reduced
     from 0.35 to 0.28 for consistent quieting.
  
  9. **Corner fill updated** (`main.rs`): Bottom-left content corner fill now
     uses BG_DARK ├ù 0.96 (was 0.90), matching the new sidebar gradient.
  
  **Design principle**: Borders and separators are visual noise. Professional apps
  like Zed rely on surface color differences (BG_DARK vs BG_BASE, gradient
  transitions) for panel separation, reserving visible borders for interactive
  elements. This pass reduces all structural borders to near-invisibility.
  
  **Result**: The UI feels calmer and more cohesive. Panel junctions are defined
  by color difference rather than drawn lines. The breadcrumb bar is more compact.
  The status bar bottom accent stripe matches the top for symmetrical framing.
  
  **Visual comparison with reference**:
  - Sidebar flatness: Ô£ô Nearly flat background, matching Zed's restrained surfaces
  - Panel separation: Ô£ô Color difference provides separation, not drawn borders
  - Breadcrumb compactness: Ô£ô 20px approaches VS Code density
  - Window frame symmetry: Ô£ô 2px accent stripes top and bottom
  - Overall noise reduction: Ô£ô Fewer visible structural borders across all chrome
  
  **Remaining gaps vs reference (iteration 56)**:
  - Terminal content not displaying (needs daemon running)
  - Multi-pane terminal layout needs daemon content
  
  ## Iteration 57 ÔÇö Accent Color Consistency Pass
  
  **Goal**: Improve accent color continuity across all chrome elements. The
  active tab's accent color should thread consistently through the breadcrumb,
  status bar, and window frame ÔÇö creating a cohesive visual language where
  every element acknowledges the active workspace's identity color.
  
  **Changes made**:
  
  1. **Bottom accent stripe edge fades** (`ui/status_bar.rs`): Replaced the
     flat `fill` with a three-part gradient (fade-in | solid | fade-out)
     matching the top accent stripe's treatment. Also added focus-responsive
     dimming: `0.20 * breath` when focused, `0.06` when unfocused ÔÇö identical
     to the top stripe's behavior. Previously the bottom stripe was a flat
     fill at `accent * 0.5` with no focus response.
  
  2. **Content spotlight intensity** (`main.rs`): Increased the radial accent
     glow behind the welcome screen from 1.2% ÔåÆ 2.0% alpha. The warm
     accent-tinted spotlight is now more perceptible, reducing the "dark void"
     feel of the empty content area and creating a subtle focal point.
  
  3. **Breadcrumb last-segment accent tint** (`main.rs`): The pill background
     on the current directory segment now blends 8% of the active accent color
     into its fill and 15% accent into its border. This creates a subtle color
     echo of the active tab's accent, connecting the breadcrumb visually to
     the tab bar's accent language.
  
  4. **Mode pill accent border** (`ui/status_bar.rs`): The status bar's mode
     indicator pill (showing "Ready" / process name) now blends 15% of the
     active accent color into its border. This carries the accent color down
     to the bottom of the window, completing the top-to-bottom accent thread.
  
  5. **Window focus propagation** (`main.rs`, `ui/status_bar.rs`): Added
     `window_focused` field to `StatusBar` struct, updated on focus events.
     The bottom accent stripe now correctly responds to window focus/unfocus
     transitions alongside the top stripe.
  
  **Technical details**:
  - Bottom stripe gradient uses identical `s(40.0)` fade width as the top
    stripe (`accent_fade` in the window border code), ensuring visual symmetry.
  - Accent tint calculations use weighted color blending (`color_a * (1-mix) +
    accent * mix`) rather than additive, preserving the base color temperature
    while adding a controlled accent wash.
  - `window_focused` propagation through `WindowEvent::Focused` handler ensures
    both the app-level top stripe and the status-bar bottom stripe dim in sync.
  
  **Result**: The active tab's accent color now flows consistently through
  every chrome element: tab badge ÔåÆ tab accent bar ÔåÆ breadcrumb segment ÔåÆ
  mode pill border ÔåÆ top/bottom accent stripes. This creates a strong
  "workspace identity" visual language where changing tabs visually shifts
  the entire interface's accent threading.
  
  **Visual comparison with reference**:
  - Accent consistency: Ô£ô Single color threads from tab bar to status bar
  - Bottom stripe polish: Ô£ô Edge fades match top stripe for symmetrical framing
  - Content warmth: Ô£ô Stronger spotlight reduces empty-area darkness
  - Breadcrumb integration: Ô£ô Active segment echoes tab accent
  - Focus states: Ô£ô Top and bottom stripes dim together on unfocus
  
  **Remaining gaps vs reference (iteration 57)**:
  - Terminal content not displaying (needs daemon running)
  - Multi-pane terminal layout needs daemon content
  
  ## Iteration 58 ÔÇö Chrome Polish: Stripe Symmetry, Close Button Discovery, Dot Visibility
  
  **Goal**: Close remaining subtle visual gaps: make the top accent stripe
  symmetrical with the bottom's fade treatment, improve inactive tab close
  button discoverability, boost sidebar accent dot visibility, and add a
  warm accent frame to the content area's top edge.
  
  **Changes made**:
  
  1. **Top accent stripe edge fades** (`ui/tab_bar.rs`): Replaced the flat
     `fill` for the 2px window-top accent stripe with a three-part gradient
     (fade-in | solid | fade-out), matching the bottom stripe's treatment
     in `ui/status_bar.rs`. The fade width is `s(40.0)` on each side, and
     the stripe alpha now modulates with the breathing glow (`0.85 * breath`)
     for a living window frame. Previously the top stripe was a flat fill
     while the bottom used faded edges ÔÇö now both are symmetrical.
  
  2. **Inactive tab close button rest visibility** (`ui/tab_bar.rs`):
     Changed `close_fade` from `active_t.max(hover_t)` (which was 0.0 for
     untouched inactive tabs) to `active_t.max(hover_t).max(0.18)`. This
     means inactive tabs always show a very faint (18% alpha) close button
     at rest, eliminating the need to hover to discover the close target.
     Professional apps (Zed, VS Code) always show close buttons on all tabs.
  
  3. **Sidebar accent dot brightness** (`ui/sidebar.rs`): Increased the
     rest-state alpha of per-session accent dots from 0.50 ÔåÆ 0.62. The
     dots are now clearly visible at rest without hover, making session
     color identity immediately apparent in the sidebar. Active/hover peak
     raised from 0.90 ÔåÆ 0.92 for consistency.
  
  4. **Content area accent frame** (`main.rs`): Added a 2px accent-tinted
     gradient at the top edge of the terminal content area. Uses the active
     tab's accent color at 6% ÔåÆ 0% alpha (top ÔåÆ bottom), creating a warm
     glow that visually connects the tab bar's accent indicator to the
     content below it. This reduces the hard boundary between tab bar and
     content, giving the content area a subtle "lit from above" feel.
  
  **Technical details**:
  - Top stripe gradient uses the same `s(40.0)` fade width as the bottom
    stripe (`bot_accent_fade` in status_bar.rs), ensuring pixel-identical
    edge treatment on both window edges.
  - Close button `0.18` rest alpha was chosen to be perceivable on both
    active and inactive tab backgrounds without being visually heavy.
    The icon alpha is `icon_color_base[3] * close_fade` where `close_fade`
    now has a floor of 0.18, so the icon is very muted but present.
  - Content accent frame gradient is only 2px tall ÔÇö just enough to create
    a subtle glow effect without being a visible colored bar.
  
  **Result**: The window frame is now perfectly symmetrical (both stripes
  have matching edge fades and breathing). Close buttons are discoverable
  on all tabs without hover. Sidebar accent dots are clearly visible at
  rest. The content area has a warmer feel with the accent top-edge glow.
  
  **Visual comparison with reference**:
  - Window frame symmetry: Ô£ô Top and bottom stripes identical treatment
  - Close button discovery: Ô£ô Always-visible close matches Zed/VS Code convention
  - Sidebar color coding: Ô£ô Session dots visible at rest like opensessions
  - Content warmth: Ô£ô Accent glow reduces tab-content boundary harshness
  
  **Remaining gaps vs reference (iteration 58)**:
  - Terminal content not displaying (needs daemon running)
  - Multi-pane terminal layout needs daemon content
  
  ## Iteration 59 ÔÇö Empty State Polish & Brand Identity
  
  **Goal**: Polish empty/idle states and reinforce brand identity through
  subtle visual refinements across the status bar, title bar, sidebar
  footer, and active workspace indicator.
  
  **Changes made**:
  
  1. **Empty status bar state** (`iced-shell/src/status_bar.rs`): When no
     terminal is focused, the status bar now shows a branded "Ready" state
     instead of being empty. A small accent-colored dot (6px, 55% alpha)
     followed by "Ready" label sits on the left, and "Godly Terminal"
     branding (60% muted) sits on the right. This matches how professional
     apps (VS Code, Zed) always populate the status bar with useful info.
  
  2. **Title bar icon accent tint** (`iced-shell/src/title_bar.rs`): The
     terminal icon in the title bar now blends 35% accent color with the
     base TEXT_SECONDARY. This gives the icon a subtle blue tint that
     reinforces brand identity without being garish. Previously the icon
     was plain muted gray.
  
  3. **Sidebar footer document icons** (`iced-shell/src/sidebar.rs`): Added
     codicon file icons (EB60) to "Project CLAUDE.md" and "User CLAUDE.md"
     footer buttons. The buttons now show `[file icon] Project CLAUDE.md`
     in a row layout with 6px spacing. This matches the icon-label pattern
     used throughout the sidebar.
  
  4. **Active workspace accent bar glow** (`iced-shell/src/sidebar.rs`):
     The active workspace's left accent bar now casts a rightward glow
     shadow (accent color at 35% alpha, 1px offset, 6px blur). This adds
     depth to the accent indicator and makes it feel like it's emitting
     light into the sidebar, similar to how Zed's active indicators glow.
  
  **Technical details**:
  - Status bar empty state uses early return pattern to avoid constructing
    the full shell/CWD/dims layout when no info is available.
  - Title bar icon color: `base * 0.65 + accent * 0.35` blend ratio chosen
    to be noticeable but not overpowering.
  - ICON_FILE constant uses codicon `\u{EB60}` (file document icon).
  - Accent bar shadow only renders when `is_active` is true, avoiding
    unnecessary shadow computation on inactive workspaces.
  
  **Result**: The app now feels more polished in its idle/empty state. The
  status bar communicates readiness rather than emptiness. The title bar
  has stronger brand identity. The sidebar footer buttons are more
  scannable with icons. The active workspace indicator has more depth.
  
  **Visual comparison with reference**:
  - Status bar populated at all times: Ô£ô Matches VS Code/Zed convention
  - Brand presence in title bar: Ô£ô Accent-tinted icon for identity
  - Icon-label pattern in sidebar: Ô£ô Consistent with settings/add buttons
  - Active indicator depth: Ô£ô Glow matches opensessions active states
  
  **Remaining gaps vs reference (iteration 59)**:
  - Terminal content not displaying (needs daemon running)
  - Multi-pane terminal layout needs daemon content
  
  ## Iteration 60 ÔÇö Visual Quieting: Hairline Separators, Compact Sidebar, Hero Refinement
  
  **Goal**: Continue the subtractive refinement toward Zed's "quiet confidence"
  aesthetic. Modernize remaining embossed grooves to single hairlines, tighten
  sidebar item layout, and refine the welcome hero icon pill.
  
  **Changes made**:
  
  1. **Tab separators modernized** (`ui/tab_bar.rs`): Replaced embossed groove
     separators (`vgroove_fade` with dark+light pair) between inactive tabs
     with single thin hairline separators (`vline_fade` at 0.15 alpha). Longer
     fade radius (6ÔåÆ8px) and taller inset (6ÔåÆ8px top/bottom) for softer edges.
     This is the last embossed groove in the tab bar ÔÇö all separators now use
     the modern single-line approach.
  
  2. **Sidebar dot column tightened** (`ui/sidebar.rs`): Reduced `dot_space`
     from 14px (11px icon + 3px gap, leftover from when items had mini terminal
     icons) to 11px (7px dot + 4px gap). This matches the actual 7px accent
     dot size and gives ~3px more room per session for name text. The session
     number x-offset now uses `dot_space` directly instead of hardcoded offsets.
  
  3. **Inactive tab rest border softened** (`ui/tab_bar.rs`): Reduced rest-state
     border alpha from 0.28 to 0.22. Tab shapes remain visible but recede
     further into the background, matching the quieter separator aesthetic.
  
  4. **Window control separator softened** (`ui/tab_bar.rs`): Reduced vertical
     separator alpha before minimize/maximize/close buttons from 0.30 to 0.20,
     and extended fade radius from 6ÔåÆ8px. Consistent with the softer separator
     language across all chrome.
  
  5. **Settings row separator modernized** (`ui/sidebar.rs`): Replaced embossed
     groove (`hgroove_fade` with dark+light pair) above the Settings row with
     a single thin hairline (`hline_fade` at 0.12 alpha, 12px fade). This was
     the last embossed groove in the sidebar ÔÇö all separators now use the
     modern single-line approach.
  
  6. **Hero icon pill tightened** (`main.rs`): Reduced pill padding from 12px
     to 10px and corner radius from 16px to 14px. The tighter composition
     makes the hero icon feel more confident and less padded.
  
  **Design principle**: This iteration eliminates the last embossed groove
  separators from both the tab bar and sidebar. Every structural separator
  in the UI now uses the single-hairline approach established in iterations
  44 and 56. The cumulative effect is a calmer, more cohesive visual language
  where all panel boundaries speak the same visual dialect.
  
  **Result**: Tab bar separators are cleaner. Sidebar sessions have more room
  for names. The hero icon is more compact and refined. All structural
  separators now use consistent single-hairline styling throughout the entire
  UI chrome.
  
  **Visual comparison with reference**:
  - Tab separators: Ô£ô Single hairlines match Zed's restrained separator style
  - Sidebar compactness: Ô£ô Tighter dot column, more name space
  - Inactive tab subtlety: Ô£ô Softer rest borders reduce visual noise
  - Settings separator: Ô£ô Modern hairline replaces dated groove emboss
  - Hero icon refinement: Ô£ô Tighter pill for confident, compact presentation
  - Separator consistency: Ô£ô All chrome uses identical hairline language
  
  **Remaining gaps vs reference (iteration 60)**:
  - Terminal content not displaying (needs daemon running)
  - Multi-pane terminal layout needs daemon content
  
  ## Iteration 61 ÔÇö Full-width CTA, Shortcut-CTA Separator, Sidebar List Fade
  
  **Goal**: Improve welcome screen CTA visual weight, add visual section clarity
  between shortcuts and action area, and add professional scroll-fade to sidebar.
  
  **Changes made**:
  
  1. **Full-width CTA button** (`main.rs`): The "Create terminal" button now
     spans the full card container width instead of being sized to content.
     Corner radius changed from `cta_h / 2.0` (pill shape) to `s(6.0)`
     (rounded rect) for a more professional full-width button appearance.
     Plus icon and label text are centered within the wider button. This
     matches professional welcome pages where the primary CTA uses the
     full available width for commanding visual weight.
  
  2. **Shortcut-CTA separator** (`main.rs`): Added a thin hairline separator
     (0.15 alpha, 60% of grid width, 12px edge fade) between the shortcut
     card container and the version/CTA area. This creates a clear visual
     break between the informational section (keyboard shortcuts) and the
     action section (version indicator + CTA button). The separator uses
     the same hairline styling as all other structural separators in the UI.
  
  3. **Sidebar session list bottom fade** (`sidebar.rs`): Added a 16px
     gradient fade at the bottom of the session list area (from 0% to 50%
     alpha BG_DARK overlay). This creates a smooth visual endpoint for the
     session list before the "New Session" button, matching how professional
     sidebars (Zed, VS Code) indicate scrollable content areas.
  
  **Technical details**:
  - CTA width: `container_rect.width` (matches card container exactly)
  - CTA x-position: `container_rect.x` (left-aligned with container)
  - Content centering: `content_x = cta_x + (cta_w - content_w) / 2.0`
  - Separator width: `grid_w * 0.6` (proportional to shortcut grid)
  - Fade gradient: 16px tall, positioned at bottom of last session item
  - Fade leaves 6px clearance for scrollbar track on right side
  
  **Result**: The welcome screen has a more commanding CTA button that reads
  as the obvious primary action. The separator creates clear information
  hierarchy: shortcuts ÔåÆ divider ÔåÆ version ÔåÆ CTA. The sidebar session list
  has a polished visual endpoint.
  
  **Visual comparison with reference**:
  - CTA visual weight: Ô£ô Full-width button matches professional welcome pages
  - Section clarity: Ô£ô Separator divides info from action cleanly
  - Sidebar polish: Ô£ô Fade gradient adds professional scroll indication
  
  **Remaining gaps vs reference (iteration 61)**:
  - Terminal content not displaying (needs daemon running)
  - Multi-pane terminal layout needs daemon content

  +## Changes in Iteration 83
  +
  +1. **The native diff now measures the same scene as `web-reference.png`** ÔÇö Added a dedicated reference scene mode in [`main.rs`], backed by a deterministic transcript renderer in [`ui/reference_pane.rs`]. This bypasses the live daemon/PTy path and removes the old ÔÇ£PowerShell prompt vs. README transcriptÔÇØ mismatch that was dominating the previous comparison.
  +2. **Retained layout now supports the cropped screenshot composition** ÔÇö [`ui/layout.rs`] can hide the status bar, and [`ui/tab_bar_layout.rs`] / [`ui/tab_bar.rs`] can hide branding, optional right-side sections, and window controls. The native parity scene now matches the cropped web screenshot instead of the full shell viewport.
  +3. **Sidebar/footer chrome is no longer polluting the crop** ÔÇö [`ui/sidebar.rs`] gained a footer-visibility toggle so the parity scene can match the screenshotÔÇÖs empty lower sidebar instead of rendering the process list and action strip that are outside the captured crop.
  +4. **One-command native launch for parity checks** ÔÇö [`scripts/measure-godly-shell-parity.ps1`] now launches the built `godly-native.exe` in `--web-reference-crop` mode automatically when no PID is provided, then captures and diff-checks that scene.
  +5. **Measured baseline after the reference-scene pass** ÔÇö Fresh same-size runs now report `Changed: 2,073,598 px (99.9999%)`, `Pixels over 10: 456,403 px (22.0102%)`, `MAE: 0.061262`, `RMSE: 0.139698`, `Max channel diff: 249`. This is materially better than the old live-shell comparison, but the remaining visible mismatch is now mostly real typography/spacing drift.
  +6. **Critical finding corrected** ÔÇö `docs/references/web-reference.png` is a cropped top-left capture of the web prototype, not the full prototype viewport. The native parity target therefore must hide the right panel, lower sidebar footer, and bottom status chrome for the screenshot mode even though those elements still exist in the full prototype.
  +
  +## Changes in Iteration 82
  +
  +1. **Physical-pixel presentation blocker closed** ÔÇö [`main.rs`] now sizes the wgpu surface from the real Win32 client rect via `GetClientRect` instead of trusting the stale logical-sized `winit` dimensions under 150% DPI. The prior behavior painted only the top-left logical region inside a `1920├ù1080` client, leaving a large black lower/right area in captures and on screen.
  +2. **Resize path now uses the same physical client query** ÔÇö The Windows resize handler no longer configures the surface directly from `WindowEvent::Resized`. It now re-queries the HWND client rect and uses that physical size, keeping live resize behavior aligned with the corrected startup path.
  +3. **Client/window capture is deterministic by default** ÔÇö [`scripts/take-screenshot-now.ps1`] now auto-enables `PrintWindow` for `-ClientOnly` / `-WindowOnly` captures unless explicitly overridden. The loopÔÇÖs default native artifact path is now window-targeted instead of depending on which desktop surface happened to be visible.
  +4. **Web reference refresh path is no longer brittle** ÔÇö [`scripts/capture-web-reference.ps1`] still verifies the expected DOM tokens and viewport, but it now uses the `browser-use screenshot` subcommand to write the PNG directly. This removes the failing Python base64 file-write path that was breaking `measure-godly-shell-parity.ps1`.
  +5. **Measured baseline after the physical-surface fix** ÔÇö Fresh `1920├ù1080` parity runs now diff at `Changed: 2,071,780 px (99.9122%)`, `Pixels over 10: 1,132,168 px (54.5992%)`, `MAE: 0.062596`, `RMSE: 0.123352`, `Max channel diff: 253`. The absolute parity gap is still large, but the black unused client region is gone and the error budget now reflects real layout/content differences rather than a broken presentation path.
  +6. **Verification** ÔÇö `cargo check -p godly-shell`, `cargo build -p godly-shell`, `cargo test -p godly-shell ui::layout -- --nocapture`, `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/capture-web-reference.ps1`, and `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/measure-godly-shell-parity.ps1 -ProcessId <pid>` all passed with the updated scripts/code.
  +
  +## Changes in Iteration 81
  +
  +1. **Retained tab-bar layout landed** ÔÇö Added [`ui/tab_bar_layout.rs`] to move the tab barÔÇÖs main horizontal structure onto a persistent `taffy` tree. The sidebar brand block, tab strip, new-tab slot, process-indicator reserve, controls gap, and window controls no longer depend on a single handwritten reserve/origin formula in [`ui/tab_bar.rs`].
  +2. **Tab-bar hit testing now uses the retained geometry** ÔÇö [`ui/tab_bar.rs`] now asks the layout engine for tab, button, and new-tab rectangles during both render and hover/click handling. This removes a second copy of the bar geometry and makes the `+` button produce `UiAction::NewTab` instead of being paint-only.
  +3. **Deterministic 1920├ù1080 web capture added** ÔÇö [`scripts/capture-web-reference.ps1`] now starts the local Vite reference when needed and uses the `browser-use` session API plus the underlying page viewport to save an exact `1920├ù1080` web screenshot to [`docs/references/web-reference.png`].
  +4. **Deterministic 1920├ù1080 native client capture added** ÔÇö [`scripts/take-screenshot-now.ps1`] now supports `-ClientOnly -ClientWidth <w> -ClientHeight <h>`, restores/resizes the shell window to an exact client area, captures it with `PrintWindow`, and crops to the client rect so shadows and invisible frame math no longer pollute the parity image.
  +5. **One-command parity measurement path added** ÔÇö [`scripts/measure-godly-shell-parity.ps1`] now refreshes both captures and runs [`scripts/check-pixels.ps1`] against them, which gives the loop a reproducible same-size visual baseline instead of the previous `2560├ù1440` vs `2582├ù1390` mismatch.
  +6. **Measured baseline after normalization** ÔÇö Fresh `1920├ù1080` captures currently diff at `Changed: 2,073,600 px (100%)`, `Pixels over 10: 2,071,289 px (99.8886%)`, `MAE: 0.124377`, `RMSE: 0.132828`, `Max channel diff: 241`. The harness is now honest and comparable; parity itself is still far away.
  +7. **Verification** ÔÇö `cargo check -p godly-shell`, `cargo test -p godly-shell ui::tab_bar_layout -- --nocapture`, `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/capture-web-reference.ps1`, `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/take-screenshot-now.ps1 -ClientOnly -ClientWidth 1920 -ClientHeight 1080`, and `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/measure-godly-shell-parity.ps1 -ProcessId <pid>` all passed.
  +
  +## Changes in Iteration 80
  +
  +1. **Real DirectWrite UI text layout path** ÔÇö Added [`ui/text_layout.rs`] to cache DirectWrite measurements for sans and serif UI runs. `UiTextRenderer` now queries that engine for widths and per-glyph x offsets instead of relying on `ui_char_advances`, average-advance estimates, or manual truncation math.
  +2. **Chrome text rendering now respects font roles and italics** ÔÇö `TextCommand` now carries a real font role (`terminal`, `ui sans`, `ui serif`) plus italic state. `TerminalRenderer` selects the matching rasterizer, so the poem panel now renders through a real serif italic face instead of the old synthetic skew.
  +3. **Background-aware text compositing landed** ÔÇö `TextCommand` also now carries a compositing mode. Flat opaque chrome surfaces use ClearType-style subpixel blending against their actual background color, while mixed surfaces (welcome hero, breadcrumb gradient) explicitly opt into grayscale AA.
  +4. **DirectWrite advance hacks removed from the frame build** ÔÇö `main.rs` no longer builds the old `ui_char_advances` table, and the builder helpers now attach real glyph positions directly to text commands. This materially closes the faux-layout blocker called out in the previous iteration.
  +5. **Screenshot helper gained deterministic window capture** ÔÇö `scripts/take-screenshot-now.ps1` now supports `-WindowOnly`, using `PrintWindow` to capture the godly-shell window directly when full-desktop capture is polluted by whichever app Windows keeps in the foreground.
  +6. **Verification** ÔÇö `cargo check -p godly-shell`, `cargo test -p godly-shell ui::layout -- --nocapture`, and `cargo test -p godly-shell ui::builder -- --nocapture` all passed. A fresh native window capture was saved to `docs/references/current-godly-shell.png` using the new `-WindowOnly` mode.
  +
  +## Changes in Iteration 79
  +
  +1. **Top-level shell layout moved onto retained `taffy` nodes** ÔÇö Replaced the hand-written `ShellLayout::compute()` region solver with a persistent `ShellLayoutEngine` backed by a `taffy::TaffyTree`. The shell now computes tab bar/body/sidebar/center/right panel/status bar through flex layout and only derives `terminal_content` as a post-layout inset. This closes the highest-leverage rectangle-math blocker at the outer shell layer without rewriting every widget in one pass.
  +2. **App render/input path now routes through the retained layout engine** ÔÇö `main.rs` holds a `RefCell<ShellLayoutEngine>` and all region queries (`terminal_size`, render, hover, resize-handle hit testing, selection routing) now pull from the same retained layout tree instead of rebuilding top-level geometry ad hoc.
  +3. **Layout verification added** ÔÇö `cargo test -p godly-shell ui::layout -- --nocapture` now exercises the retained layout engine for visible, hidden, and scaled configurations so layout regressions are caught before screenshot comparison.
  +4. **Screenshot-diff harness is now measurable** ÔÇö `scripts/check-pixels.ps1` was rewritten to compute full-image metrics (`changed_pixels`, `pixels_over_10`, `max_channel_diff`, `MAE`, `RMSE`) and emit `current-godly-shell.diff.png`. This replaces the previous hard-coded spot sampling script.
  +5. **Native screenshot capture is now DPI-aware** ÔÇö `scripts/take-screenshot-now.ps1` now calls `SetProcessDpiAwarenessContext(PER_MONITOR_AWARE_V2)` before reading screen bounds, so captured screenshots are in physical pixels (`2560x1440` here) rather than logical pixels (`1707x960`).
  +6. **Measured baseline after the harness landed** ÔÇö Fresh captures at `2560x1440` currently diff at `Changed: 3,686,400 px (100%)`, `Pixels over 10: 3,684,752 px (99.9553%)`, `MAE: 0.350239`, `RMSE: 0.500536`, `Max channel diff: 245`. The harness is working, and it confirms parity is still far away.
  +
   ## Changes in Iteration 78
   
   1. **FG_INACTIVE color constant** ÔÇö Added `#9198a1` ([0.569, 0.596, 0.631]) as `FG_INACTIVE` to the color palette. Web uses this for inactive session names, agent names, and poem stanzas. Previously these elements used `FG_SECONDARY` (#8b949e) which was slightly too dark.

   ## Remaining Gaps (Priority Order)
   
  -### Future: HiDPI Rendering
  -The surface renders at logical resolution (1707├ù912) and the compositor upscales to
  -physical (2560├ù1440). Text is rendered at 1x resolution rather than native HiDPI.
  -
  -**Investigation (iteration 74):** Tested DX12 backend with physical resolution surface
  -(2560├ù1368) and explicit `SetProcessDpiAwarenessContext(PER_MONITOR_AWARE_V2)`. The DWM
  -compositor still clips swap chain content at the logical pixel boundary (1707px),
  -confirmed via pixel analysis. This is a Windows DWM limitation ÔÇö the compositor
  -presents only logical-width pixels of the swap chain regardless of GPU backend or DPI
  -awareness. Fixing this likely requires a different surface presentation strategy
  -(e.g., off-screen render target + blit, or Win32 API-level DXGI swap chain control
  -bypassing wgpu's abstraction).
  +### Critical: Transcript typography and spacing still diverge from the browser capture
  +The native reference scene now shows the correct content, but the central pane is still not
  +at parity on vertical rhythm, glyph weight, and exact run widths. The remaining diff is no
  +longer dominated by the wrong scene; it is concentrated in the visible text itself.
  +
  +**Next measurable step:** tighten the transcript pane against the web capture using a
  +dedicated browser-matching monospace rasterizer/layout path (or an objectively equivalent
  +DirectWrite configuration), then retune line spacing and inline-run placement from the new
  +baseline.
  +
  +### Major: The visible tab-strip geometry is still off
  +The cropped parity mode removed the off-frame controls, but the tab widths, label spacing,
  +and badge positions still do not line up exactly with the browser capture.
  +
  +**Next layout step:** tune the retained tab-strip sizing for the cropped target, including
  +active-tab width, gap/padding, and badge placement, instead of reusing the full-shell tab
  +metrics unchanged.
  +
  +### Major: Inner chrome/content layout is still mostly manual
  +The top-level shell and optional tab-bar sections now use retained layout, but the sidebar
  +internals and the reference transcript are still hand-positioned. That is a better state
  +than iteration 82, but it is not the end state implied by the parity gates.
  +
  +**Next architectural step:** move the cropped transcript flow and sidebar session stack onto
  +a retained flex/layout layer so spacing changes stop depending on handwritten `y += ...`
  +math.
  +
  +### Medium: Measurable visual gaps remain after the scene-mode fix
  +The latest same-size run still has `22.0102%` of pixels differing by more than 10 RGB
  +levels. That is far more honest than the old live-shell diff, but it confirms parity is
  +still materially unfinished.

  +fn window_surface_size(window: &Window) -> winit::dpi::PhysicalSize<u32> {
  +    #[cfg(windows)]
  +    if let Some(size) = win32_client_size(window) {
  +        return size;
  +    }
  +
  +    window.inner_size()
  +}
  +
  +#[cfg(windows)]
  +fn win32_client_size(window: &Window) -> Option<winit::dpi::PhysicalSize<u32>> {
  +    use raw_window_handle::{HasWindowHandle, RawWindowHandle};
  +    use windows::Win32::Foundation::{HWND, RECT};
  +    use windows::Win32::UI::WindowsAndMessaging::GetClientRect;
  +
  +    let handle = window.window_handle().ok()?;
  +    let hwnd = match handle.as_raw() {
  +        RawWindowHandle::Win32(handle) => HWND(handle.hwnd.get() as *mut core::ffi::c_void),
  +        _ => return None,
  +    };
  +
  +    let mut rect = RECT::default();
  +    unsafe {
  +        if GetClientRect(hwnd, &mut rect).is_err() {
  +            return None;
  +        }
  +    }
  +
  +    let width = (rect.right - rect.left).max(0) as u32;
  +    let height = (rect.bottom - rect.top).max(0) as u32;
  +    Some(winit::dpi::PhysicalSize::new(width.max(1), height.max(1)))
   }

  +fn scene_mode_from_env_and_args() -> SceneMode {
  +    let cli_reference = std::env::args().any(|arg| arg == "--web-reference-crop");
  +    let env_reference = matches!(
  +        std::env::var("GODLY_SHELL_REFERENCE_MODE").as_deref(),
  +        Ok("web_reference_crop") | Ok("web-reference-crop") | Ok("1") | Ok("true")
  +    );
  +
  +    if cli_reference || env_reference {
  +        SceneMode::WebReferenceCrop
  +    } else {
  +        SceneMode::LiveShell
  +    }

  +        &self,
  +        font_kind: UiFontKind,
  +        s: &str,
  +        scale: f32,
  +        bold: bool,
  +        italic: bool,
  +    ) -> UiTextLayout {
  +        if let Some(engine) = &self.layout_engine {
  +            if let Some(layout) =
  +                engine.layout(font_kind, s, self.font_size_px * scale, bold, italic)

  +                return layout;
  +            }
  +        }

  +        let mut x = 0.0;
  +        for _ in s.chars() {
  +            glyph_offsets.push(x);
  +            x += advance;
  +        }
  +        UiTextLayout {
  +            width: x,
  +            line_height: self.font_size_px * scale,
  +            glyph_offsets,
  +        }
       }

  +    fn set_style(&mut self, node: NodeId, style: Style) {
  +        self.tree
  +            .set_style(node, style)
  +            .expect("layout node style should update");

  +
  +    fn absolute_rect_for(&self, path: &[NodeId]) -> Rect {
  +        let mut x = 0.0;
  +        let mut y = 0.0;
  +        let mut width = 0.0;
  +        let mut height = 0.0;
  +
  +        for node in path {
  +            let layout = self.tree.layout(*node).expect("layout node should exist");
  +            x += layout.location.x;
  +            y += layout.location.y;
  +            width = layout.size.width;
  +            height = layout.size.height;
  +        }
  +
  +        Rect {
  +            x,
  +            y,
  +            width,
  +            height,
  +        }
  +    }
  +}

  +impl Default for ShellLayoutEngine {
  +    fn default() -> Self {
  +        Self::new()
  +    }
  +}
  +
  +impl ShellLayout {
  +    /// Compatibility wrapper for older call sites and tests.
  +    pub fn compute(
  +        viewport_w: f32,
  +        viewport_h: f32,
  +        sidebar_visible: bool,
  +        right_panel_visible: bool,
  +        status_bar_visible: bool,
  +        sidebar_width: f32,
  +        right_panel_width: f32,
  +        scale: f32,
  +    ) -> Self {
  +        ShellLayoutEngine::new().compute(
  +            viewport_w,
  +            viewport_h,
  +            sidebar_visible,
  +            right_panel_visible,
  +            status_bar_visible,
  +            sidebar_width,
  +            right_panel_width,
  +            scale,
  +        )
  +    }
  +}
  +
  +fn px(value: f32) -> Dimension {
  +    Dimension::Length(value.max(0.0))
  +}
  +
  +fn hidden_style() -> Style {
  +    Style {
  +        display: Display::None,
  +        ..Default::default()
  +    }
  +}
  +
  +fn zero_rect() -> Rect {
  +    Rect {
  +        x: 0.0,
  +        y: 0.0,
  +        width: 0.0,
  +        height: 0.0,
  +    }
  +}

  +    fn approx_eq(a: f32, b: f32) {
  +        assert!(
  +            (a - b).abs() < 0.01,
  +            "expected {a} ~= {b}, diff={}",
  +            (a - b).abs()
  +        );
  +    }
  +
  +    #[test]
  +    fn taffy_layout_matches_expected_visible_regions() {
  +        let layout = ShellLayout::compute(1200.0, 800.0, true, true, true, 200.0, 380.0, 1.0);
  +
  +        approx_eq(layout.tab_bar.height, 36.0);
  +        approx_eq(layout.status_bar.y, 774.0);
  +        approx_eq(layout.sidebar.width, 200.0);
  +        approx_eq(layout.sidebar.y, 36.0);
  +        approx_eq(layout.right_panel.x, 820.0);
  +        approx_eq(layout.right_panel.width, 380.0);
  +        approx_eq(layout.terminal.x, 200.0);
  +        approx_eq(layout.terminal.y, 36.0);
  +        approx_eq(layout.terminal.width, 620.0);
  +        approx_eq(layout.terminal.height, 738.0);
  +        approx_eq(layout.terminal_content.x, 208.0);
  +        approx_eq(layout.terminal_content.y, 42.0);
  +    }
  +
  +    #[test]

  +
  +        approx_eq(layout.sidebar.width, 0.0);
  +        approx_eq(layout.right_panel.width, 0.0);
  +        approx_eq(layout.terminal.x, 0.0);
  +        approx_eq(layout.terminal.width, 1200.0);
  +    }

  +    #[test]

  +

  +## Iteration 83 ÔÇö Cropped Reference Scene Mode + Auto-Launch Harness
  +
  +**Goal**: Stop diffing the wrong scene. The target screenshot is a cropped web
  +reference, so the native shell needed a deterministic mode that matches that
  +visible composition instead of rendering a live PTY, full sidebar footer, right
  +panel, and status chrome.
  +
  +**Changes made**:
  +
  +1. **Added a deterministic web-reference crop mode**
  +   (`src-tauri/native/godly-shell/src/main.rs`,
  +   `src-tauri/native/godly-shell/src/ui/reference_pane.rs`) ÔÇö The shell now
  +   supports `--web-reference-crop` / `GODLY_SHELL_REFERENCE_MODE=web_reference_crop`.
  +   In that mode it skips the daemon-backed live terminal path and renders the
  +   visible transcript from `web-reference.png` directly through the native text
  +   pipeline, so the harness no longer compares against a PowerShell prompt or
  +   the branded empty-state hero.
  +
  +2. **Retained layout now supports the cropped screenshot composition**
  +   (`ui/layout.rs`, `ui/tab_bar_layout.rs`, `ui/tab_bar.rs`) ÔÇö The shell layout
  +   can now hide the bottom status bar, and the retained tab-bar layout can
  +   independently hide branding, right-side indicators, controls, and the new-tab
  +   slot. This makes the native scene match the cropped web capture instead of
  +   the full shell viewport.
  +
  +3. **Sidebar footer chrome can be removed for parity capture**
  +   (`ui/sidebar.rs`) ÔÇö Added a footer-visibility toggle so the lower process list
  +   and shortcuts strip do not appear in the cropped reference mode. The sidebar
  +   now leaves the same large empty lower region visible in `web-reference.png`.
  +
  +4. **Parity harness now launches the reference scene automatically**
  +   (`scripts/measure-godly-shell-parity.ps1`) ÔÇö When no PID is supplied, the
  +   measurement script launches the built `godly-native.exe` in
  +   `--web-reference-crop` mode, waits for the window, captures it, and shuts it
  +   down again. The parity loop no longer depends on a manually staged process.
  +
  +5. **Measured baseline after the scene-mode fix** ÔÇö Fresh `1920x1080` runs now
  +   report:
  +   - changed: `2,073,598 px (99.9999%)`
  +   - over 10: `456,403 px (22.0102%)`
  +   - MAE: `0.061262`
  +   - RMSE: `0.139698`
  +   - max channel diff: `249`
  +
  +**Result**: The parity harness now compares the right scene, and the remaining
  +gap is visibly concentrated in transcript typography/spacing and tab geometry
  +rather than whole missing panels or live terminal state.
  +
  +**Verification**:
  +
  +- `cargo check -p godly-shell`
  +- `cargo build -p godly-shell`
  +- `cargo test -p godly-shell ui::layout -- --nocapture`
  +- `cargo test -p godly-shell ui::tab_bar_layout -- --nocapture`
  +- `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/measure-godly-shell-parity.ps1`

  +## Iteration 82 ÔÇö Physical Client Sizing + Stable Parity Refresh
  +
  +**Goal**: Close the real presentation blocker before doing more layout polish,
  +and make the one-command parity loop reliable again after the web capture path
  +started failing.
  +
  +**Changes made**:
  +
  +1. **Physical client sizing on Windows** (`src-tauri/native/godly-shell/src/main.rs`)
  +   ÔÇö Added a Windows-only `GetClientRect` surface-size path and used it for
  +   both startup and resize configuration. The previous physical-surface attempt
  +   was still sizing from stale logical `winit` dimensions on this setup, which
  +   left the lower/right portion of a `1920x1080` client black while only the
  +   top-left logical region rendered. The shell now fills the full client again.
  +
  +2. **Resize handling now re-queries the HWND client rect**
  +   (`src-tauri/native/godly-shell/src/main.rs`) ÔÇö The resize handler no longer
  +   trusts `WindowEvent::Resized` as the final surface size under DPI scaling.
  +   It re-queries the actual client rect before reconfiguring the surface.
  +
  +3. **Deterministic client capture by default**
  +   (`scripts/take-screenshot-now.ps1`) ÔÇö `-ClientOnly` and `-WindowOnly`
  +   now default to `PrintWindow` unless `-UsePrintWindow:$false` is supplied.
  +   This aligns the helper with the docs and keeps native parity captures from
  +   depending on desktop-focus screen grabs.
  +
  +4. **Web reference refresh no longer depends on Python base64 output**
  +   (`scripts/capture-web-reference.ps1`) ÔÇö Kept the `browser-use` viewport and
  +   DOM-token verification, but switched the actual PNG write step to
  +   `browser-use screenshot`. This fixed the failure mode where the script said
  +   the browser run succeeded but never materialized `web-reference.raw.png`.
  +
  +5. **Measured baseline after the presentation fix** ÔÇö Fresh
  +   `scripts/measure-godly-shell-parity.ps1` runs now report:
  +   - changed: `2,071,780 px (99.9122%)`
  +   - over 10: `1,132,168 px (54.5992%)`
  +   - MAE: `0.062596`
  +   - RMSE: `0.123352`
  +   - max channel diff: `253`
  +
  +**Result**: The native shell now presents across the full physical client at
  +`1920x1080` instead of leaving a large black unused region, and the one-command
  +parity path works again end to end. The next biggest measured gap is no longer
  +presentation failure; it is the fact that the native main pane is still showing
  +live PowerShell content instead of the web reference transcript.
  +
  +**Verification**:
  +
  +- `cargo check -p godly-shell`
  +- `cargo build -p godly-shell`
  +- `cargo test -p godly-shell ui::layout -- --nocapture`
  +- `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/capture-web-reference.ps1`
  +- `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/measure-godly-shell-parity.ps1 -ProcessId <pid>`

  +   ÔÇö Render and hover/click handling now query the same retained layout output.

  +   restores/resizes the shell window to an exact client area, captures with

  +   frame bounds stop polluting the comparison image.

  +   ÔÇö Added an orchestration script that refreshes both captures and then runs

  +## Iteration 80 ÔÇö DirectWrite UI Layout + Background-Aware Compositing
  +
  +**Goal**: Replace the remaining faux chrome-text path with a real Windows
  +measurement/compositing path instead of spending another loop on spacing polish.
  +
  +**Changes made**:
  +
  +1. **DirectWrite UI layout cache** (`ui/text_layout.rs`, `ui/builder.rs`) ÔÇö
  +   Added a cached DirectWrite text layout engine for sans and serif UI runs.
  +   `UiTextRenderer` now asks that engine for widths and per-glyph x offsets,
  +   replacing the old `ui_char_advances` / average-advance fallback path for
  +   real UI text measurement and placement.
  +
  +2. **Real font roles and italics** (`ui/builder.rs`, `terminal_renderer.rs`,
  +   `ui/right_panel.rs`) ÔÇö `TextCommand` now carries a real font role
  +   (`terminal`, `ui sans`, `ui serif`) plus italic state. The renderer picks
  +   the matching rasterizer, and the poem panel now renders through a real
  +   serif italic face instead of synthetic skew.
  +
  +3. **Background-aware compositing** (`ui/builder.rs`, `terminal_renderer.rs`,
  +   `main.rs`) ÔÇö Text commands now choose between flat-background subpixel
  +   blending and mixed-background grayscale AA. Flat chrome surfaces (status
  +   bar, sidebar, tabs, right panel chrome) can use ClearType-style subpixel
  +   blending, while mixed surfaces such as the welcome hero and breadcrumb
  +   explicitly opt into grayscale AA.
  +
  +4. **Screenshot helper improved for real shell capture**
  +   (`scripts/take-screenshot-now.ps1`) ÔÇö Added `-WindowOnly`, which uses
  +   `PrintWindow` to capture the godly-shell window directly when full-desktop
  +   capture is contaminated by some other foreground app. This gives a
  +   deterministic native artifact even though the full-frame diff flow still
  +   needs size normalization.
  +
  +**Verification**:
  +
  +- `cargo check -p godly-shell`
  +- `cargo test -p godly-shell ui::layout -- --nocapture`
  +- `cargo test -p godly-shell ui::builder -- --nocapture`
  +- `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/take-screenshot-now.ps1 -ProcessId <pid> -WindowOnly -OutPath docs/references/current-godly-shell.png`
  +- `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/check-pixels.ps1 ...`
  +  currently fails with `Image size mismatch: reference=2560x1440, actual=2582x1390`, which is now the explicit normalization gap instead of a hidden capture failure.
  +
  +**Result**: The text path is materially better aligned with the non-negotiable
  +parity gates. The shell now has a real DirectWrite measurement path, a real
  +italic face, and a real compositing decision per text run. The remaining hard
  +blockers are still physical-pixel presentation, inner-shell retained layout,
  +and a deterministic same-size screenshot compare path.
  +
  +## Iteration 79 ÔÇö Retained Shell Layout + Real Diff Harness
  +
  +**Goal**: Stop spending iterations on top-level hand-authored rectangle math,
  +and make the screenshot comparison path measurable instead of anecdotal.

  +   the top-level manual `ShellLayout::compute()` region solver with a retained
  +   `ShellLayoutEngine` backed by `taffy::TaffyTree`. The shell regions
  +   (tab bar, body, sidebar, center column, right panel, status bar) now come
  +   from flex layout, and `main.rs` routes render/input/resize queries through
  +   the retained engine.
  +

  +   visible, hidden, and scaled configurations. Verified with:
  +   `cargo test -p godly-shell ui::layout -- --nocapture`

  +   - pixels-over-threshold counts
  +   - max channel delta
  +   - MAE / RMSE
  +   - `current-godly-shell.diff.png`
  +

  +

  +   - size: `2560x1440`

  +   - MAE: `0.350239`
  +   - RMSE: `0.500536`
  +   - max channel diff: `245`

   416:     fn draw_cursor(&self, ui: &mut UiBuilder, text: &UiTextRenderer, x: f32, y: f32) {
   417:         ui.fill_rounded(
   418:             Rect {
   419:                 x,
   420:                 y,

   429:     fn draw_inline_runs(
   430:         &self,
   431:         ui: &mut UiBuilder,
   432:         text: &UiTextRenderer,
   433:         mut x: f32,
   434:         y: f32,
   435:         runs: &[InlineRun<'_>],
   436:         scale: f32,
   437:         bg: [f32; 4],
   438:     ) {
   439:         let s = |v: f32| text.s(v);
   440:         for run in runs {
   441:             match run {
   442:                 InlineRun::Text(value) => {
   443:                     ui.text_scaled(text, value, x, y, colors::FG_PRIMARY, bg, scale);
   444:                     x += text.text_width_scaled(value, scale);
   445:                 }
   446:                 InlineRun::Link(value) => {
   447:                     ui.text_scaled(text, value, x, y, LINK_COLOR, bg, scale);
   448:                     let width = text.text_width_scaled(value, scale);
   449:                     ui.hline_aa(
   450:                         x,
   451:                         y + text.cell_height * scale + s(1.0),
   452:                         width,
   453:                         1.0,
   454:                         LINK_COLOR,
   455:                     );
   456:                     x += width;
   457:                 }
   458:                 InlineRun::Code(value) => {
   459:                     let code_scale = SMALL_SCALE;
   460:                     let pad_x = s(5.0);
   461:                     let code_w = text.text_width_scaled(value, code_scale);
   462:                     let code_h = text.cell_height * code_scale + s(4.0);
   463:                     let rect = Rect {
   464:                         x,
   465:                         y: y + s(1.0),
   466:                         width: code_w + pad_x * 2.0,
   467:                         height: code_h,
   468:                     };
   469:                     ui.fill_rounded(rect, CODE_BG, s(3.0));
   470:                     ui.text_scaled(
   471:                         text,
   472:                         value,
   473:                         rect.x + pad_x,
   474:                         rect.y + s(1.0),
   475:                         colors::ACCENT_GOLD,
   476:                         CODE_BG,
   477:                         code_scale,
   478:                     );
   479:                     x += rect.width;
   480:                 }
   481:             }
   482:         }
   483:     }
   484: }
   485: 
   486: enum InlineRun<'a> {
   487:     Text(&'a str),
   488:     Link(&'a str),
   489:     Code(&'a str),
   490: }
  
  --- chunk end ---

    12:     pub width: f32,
    13:     pub line_height: f32,
    14:     pub glyph_offsets: Vec<f32>,
    15: }
    16: 
    17: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    18: pub struct UiFontFamilies {
    19:     pub sans: String,
    20:     pub serif: String,
    21: }
    22: 
    23: #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
    24: struct LayoutKey {
    25:     font: UiFontKind,
    26:     text: String,
    27:     size_q4: u16,
    28:     bold: bool,
    29:     italic: bool,
    30: }
    31: 
    32: #[cfg(windows)]
    33: struct LayoutCache {
    34:     factory: windows::Win32::Graphics::DirectWrite::IDWriteFactory,
    35:     families: UiFontFamilies,
    36:     cache: HashMap<LayoutKey, UiTextLayout>,
    37: }
    38: 
    39: pub struct UiTextLayoutEngine {
    40:     #[cfg(windows)]
    41:     inner: RefCell<LayoutCache>,
    42: }
    43: 
    44: impl UiTextLayoutEngine {
    45:     #[cfg(windows)]
    46:     pub fn new(families: UiFontFamilies) -> windows::core::Result<Self> {
    47:         use windows::Win32::Graphics::DirectWrite::{
    48:             DWriteCreateFactory, DWRITE_FACTORY_TYPE_SHARED, IDWriteFactory,
    49:         };
    50: 
    51:         let factory: IDWriteFactory = unsafe { DWriteCreateFactory(DWRITE_FACTORY_TYPE_SHARED)? };
    52:         Ok(Self {
    53:             inner: RefCell::new(LayoutCache {
    54:                 factory,
    55:                 families,
    56:                 cache: HashMap::new(),
    57:             }),
    58:         })
    59:     }
    60: 
    61:     #[cfg(not(windows))]
    62:     pub fn new(_families: UiFontFamilies) -> Result<Self, ()> {
    63:         Ok(Self {})
    64:     }
    65: 
    66:     pub fn layout(
    67:         &self,
    68:         font: UiFontKind,
    69:         text: &str,
    70:         font_size_px: f32,
    71:         bold: bool,
    72:         italic: bool,
    73:     ) -> Option<UiTextLayout> {
    74:         #[cfg(windows)]
    75:         {
    76:             let key = LayoutKey {
    77:                 font,
    78:                 text: text.to_string(),
    79:                 size_q4: (font_size_px.max(0.0) * 4.0).round() as u16,
    80:                 bold,
    81:                 italic,
    82:             };
    83:             let mut inner = self.inner.borrow_mut();
    84:             if let Some(layout) = inner.cache.get(&key) {
    85:                 return Some(layout.clone());
    86:             }
    87:             let layout = compute_layout(&inner.factory, &inner.families, &key)?;
    88:             inner.cache.insert(key, layout.clone());
    89:             Some(layout)
    90:         }
    91:         #[cfg(not(windows))]
    92:         {
    93:             let _ = (font, text, font_size_px, bold, italic);
    94:             None
    95:         }
    96:     }
    97: }
    98: 
    99: #[cfg(windows)]
   100: fn compute_layout(
   101:     factory: &windows::Win32::Graphics::DirectWrite::IDWriteFactory,
   102:     families: &UiFontFamilies,
   103:     key: &LayoutKey,
   104: ) -> Option<UiTextLayout> {
   105:     use windows::core::PCWSTR;
   106:     use windows::Win32::Graphics::DirectWrite::{
   107:         DWRITE_FONT_STRETCH_NORMAL, DWRITE_FONT_STYLE_ITALIC, DWRITE_FONT_STYLE_NORMAL,
   108:         DWRITE_FONT_WEIGHT_BOLD, DWRITE_FONT_WEIGHT_REGULAR, DWRITE_HIT_TEST_METRICS,
   109:         DWRITE_TEXT_METRICS, DWRITE_WORD_WRAPPING_NO_WRAP,
   110:     };
   111: 
   112:     let family = match key.font {
   113:         UiFontKind::Sans => &families.sans,
   114:         UiFontKind::Serif => &families.serif,
   115:     };
   116:     let family_wide = wide(family);
   117:     let locale = wide("en-US");
   118:     let utf16: Vec<u16> = key.text.encode_utf16().collect();
   119: 
   120:     let weight = if key.bold {
   121:         DWRITE_FONT_WEIGHT_BOLD
   122:     } else {
   123:         DWRITE_FONT_WEIGHT_REGULAR
   124:     };
   125:     let style = if key.italic {
   126:         DWRITE_FONT_STYLE_ITALIC
   127:     } else {
   128:         DWRITE_FONT_STYLE_NORMAL
   129:     };
   130:     let font_size_px = key.size_q4 as f32 / 4.0;
   131: 
   132:     let format = unsafe {
   133:         factory
   134:             .CreateTextFormat(
   135:                 PCWSTR(family_wide.as_ptr()),
   136:                 None,
   137:                 weight,
   138:                 style,
   139:                 DWRITE_FONT_STRETCH_NORMAL,
   140:                 font_size_px,
   141:                 PCWSTR(locale.as_ptr()),
   142:             )
   143:             .ok()?
   144:     };
   145:     let _ = unsafe { format.SetWordWrapping(DWRITE_WORD_WRAPPING_NO_WRAP) };
   146: 
   147:     let layout = unsafe {
   148:         factory
   149:             .CreateTextLayout(&utf16, &format, 4096.0, 4096.0)
   150:             .ok()?
   151:     };
   152: 
   153:     let mut metrics = DWRITE_TEXT_METRICS::default();
   154:     unsafe { layout.GetMetrics(&mut metrics).ok()? };
   155: 
   156:     let mut glyph_offsets = Vec::with_capacity(key.text.chars().count());
   157:     let mut utf16_index = 0u32;
   158:     for ch in key.text.chars() {
   159:         let mut x = 0.0f32;
   160:         let mut y = 0.0f32;
   161:         let mut hit = DWRITE_HIT_TEST_METRICS::default();
   162:         unsafe {
   163:             layout
   164:                 .HitTestTextPosition(utf16_index, false, &mut x, &mut y, &mut hit)
   165:                 .ok()?;
   166:         }
   167:         glyph_offsets.push(x);
   168:         utf16_index += ch.len_utf16() as u32;
   169:     }
   170: 
   171:     Some(UiTextLayout {
   172:         width: metrics.widthIncludingTrailingWhitespace.max(metrics.width),
   173:         line_height: metrics.height.max(font_size_px),
   174:         glyph_offsets,
   175:     })
   176: }
   177: 
   178: #[cfg(windows)]
   179: fn wide(value: &str) -> Vec<u16> {
   180:     value.encode_utf16().chain(std::iter::once(0)).collect()
   181: }

    50: /// Font size scale factors relative to the 14px base cell height.
    51: /// Used with `text_ui_scaled`/`text_ui_bold_scaled` to match web reference pixel sizes.
    52: pub mod font_scale {
    53:     pub const PX9: f32 = 9.0 / 14.0;   // 0.643 ÔÇö tab count badges
    54:     pub const PX10: f32 = 10.0 / 14.0;  // 0.714 ÔÇö shortcuts, badges, small headers
    55:     pub const PX11: f32 = 11.0 / 14.0;  // 0.786 ÔÇö branch text, descriptions, status bar
    56:     pub const PX12: f32 = 12.0 / 14.0;  // 0.857 ÔÇö session header, tab titles, process names
    57:     pub const PX13: f32 = 13.0 / 14.0;  // 0.929 ÔÇö session names, poem stanzas
    58:     pub const PX15: f32 = 15.0 / 14.0;  // 1.071 ÔÇö poem title
    59: }

    61: /// A deferred text draw command. Characters are rasterized through the
    62: /// glyph atlas at render time so UI chrome gets the same ClearType quality
    63: /// as terminal content.
    64: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    65: pub struct TextCommand {
    66:     pub text: String,
    67:     pub x: f32,
    68:     pub y: f32,
    69:     pub fg: [f32; 4],
    70:     pub bg: [f32; 4],
    71:     /// When true, renders glyphs with the bold font variant.
    72:     pub bold: bool,
    73:     /// Which rasterizer/layout path should be used for this text run.
    74:     pub font_kind: TextFontKind,
    75:     /// When true, renders with the italic font face instead of a synthetic skew.
    76:     pub italic: bool,
    77:     /// Scale factor for glyph quads (1.0 = normal size, 0.786 = 11/14px).
    78:     pub scale: f32,
    79:     /// Glyph x offsets from a real text layout pass. Empty for monospace runs.
    80:     pub glyph_offsets: Vec<f32>,
    81:     /// Whether the renderer can use subpixel blending against `bg`, or must
    82:     /// fall back to grayscale AA because the backdrop is mixed/unknown.
    83:     pub composite: TextCompositeMode,
    84: }
    85: 
    86: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
    87: pub enum TextFontKind {
    88:     TerminalMono,
    89:     UiSans,
    90:     UiSerif,
    91: }
    92: 
    93: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
    94: pub enum TextCompositeMode {
    95:     FlatBackground,
    96:     MixedBackground,
    97: }
    98: 
    99: /// Thin handle passed to widget `build()` methods. Carries actual cell
   100: /// dimensions from the font metrics so widgets can position text correctly.
   101: pub struct UiTextRenderer {
   102:     pub cell_width: f32,
   103:     pub cell_height: f32,
   104:     pub font_size_px: f32,
   105:     pub scale: f32,
   106:     /// Average advance width of the proportional UI font (0 if unavailable).
   107:     pub ui_avg_advance: f32,
   108:     /// Optional real UI text layout path. When present, widths and glyph
   109:     /// positions come from DirectWrite instead of hand-tuned advance math.
   110:     pub layout_engine: Option<Rc<UiTextLayoutEngine>>,
   111: }
   112: 
   113: impl UiTextRenderer {
   114:     pub fn new(cell_width: f32, cell_height: f32, font_size_px: f32, scale: f32) -> Self {
   115:         Self {
   116:             cell_width,
   117:             cell_height,
   118:             font_size_px,
   119:             scale,
   120:             ui_avg_advance: 0.0,
   121:             layout_engine: None,
   122:         }
   123:     }
   124: 
   125:     /// Scale a logical pixel value to physical pixels.
   126:     pub fn s(&self, v: f32) -> f32 {
   127:         (v * self.scale).round()
   128:     }
   129: 
   130:     /// Width in pixels of a string rendered in the terminal monospace font.
   131:     pub fn text_width(&self, s: &str) -> f32 {
   132:         s.chars().count() as f32 * self.cell_width
   133:     }
   134: 
   135:     /// Width in pixels of a string rendered in the terminal monospace font at a given scale.
   136:     pub fn text_width_scaled(&self, s: &str, scale: f32) -> f32 {
   137:         self.text_width(s) * scale
   138:     }
   139: 
   140:     /// Exact width of a string rendered in the proportional UI font.
   141:     pub fn text_width_ui(&self, s: &str) -> f32 {
   142:         self.layout_text(UiFontKind::Sans, s, 1.0, false, false)
   143:             .width
   144:     }
   145: 
   146:     /// Exact width of a string rendered in the proportional UI font at a given scale.
   147:     pub fn text_width_ui_scaled(&self, s: &str, scale: f32) -> f32 {
   148:         self.layout_text(UiFontKind::Sans, s, scale, false, false)
   149:             .width
   150:     }
   151: 
   152:     /// Exact width of a string rendered in the serif UI font at a given scale.
   153:     pub fn text_width_serif_scaled(
   154:         &self,
   155:         s: &str,
   156:         scale: f32,
   157:         bold: bool,
   158:         italic: bool,
   159:     ) -> f32 {
   160:         self.layout_text(UiFontKind::Serif, s, scale, bold, italic)
   161:             .width
   162:     }
   163: 
   164:     pub fn glyph_offsets_ui(
   165:         &self,
   166:         s: &str,
   167:         scale: f32,
   168:         bold: bool,
   169:         italic: bool,
   170:         font_kind: TextFontKind,
   171:     ) -> Vec<f32> {
   172:         match font_kind {
   173:             TextFontKind::TerminalMono => Vec::new(),
   174:             TextFontKind::UiSans => self
   175:                 .layout_text(UiFontKind::Sans, s, scale, bold, italic)
   176:                 .glyph_offsets,
   177:             TextFontKind::UiSerif => self
   178:                 .layout_text(UiFontKind::Serif, s, scale, bold, italic)
   179:                 .glyph_offsets,
   180:         }
   181:     }
   182: 
   183:     fn layout_text(
   184:         &self,
   185:         font_kind: UiFontKind,
   186:         s: &str,
   187:         scale: f32,
   188:         bold: bool,
   189:         italic: bool,
   190:     ) -> UiTextLayout {
   191:         if let Some(engine) = &self.layout_engine {
   192:             if let Some(layout) =
   193:                 engine.layout(font_kind, s, self.font_size_px * scale, bold, italic)
   194:             {
   195:                 return layout;
   196:             }
   197:         }
   198: 
   199:         let advance = if self.ui_avg_advance > 0.0 {
   200:             self.ui_avg_advance * scale
   201:         } else {
   202:             self.cell_width * 0.75 * scale
   203:         };
   204:         let mut glyph_offsets = Vec::with_capacity(s.chars().count());
   205:         let mut x = 0.0;
   206:         for _ in s.chars() {
   207:             glyph_offsets.push(x);
   208:             x += advance;
   209:         }
   210:         UiTextLayout {
   211:             width: x,
   212:             line_height: self.font_size_px * scale,
   213:             glyph_offsets,
   214:         }
   215:     }
   216: }

    38:         let phys = font_metrics.scaled_for_render();
    39:         let glyph_atlas = GlyphAtlas::new(phys.cell_width, phys.cell_height, phys.baseline_offset);

    54:     /// Set the proportional UI font rasterizer (e.g., Segoe UI).
    55:     pub fn set_ui_rasterizer(&mut self, mut rasterizer: Box<dyn GlyphRasterizer>) {

    57:         let metrics = rasterizer.measure(phys.font_size);

    71:     /// Prepare GPU resources (atlas texture upload, vertex buffer) BEFORE the render pass.
    72:     ///
    73:     /// Renders both terminal grid content and UI chrome text through the
    74:     /// same glyph atlas pipeline for consistent ClearType quality.
    75:     pub fn prepare(
    76:         &mut self,
    77:         device: &wgpu::Device,
    78:         queue: &wgpu::Queue,
    79:         grid: Option<&RichGridData>,
    80:         full_width: u32,
    81:         full_height: u32,
    82:         offset_x: f32,
    83:         offset_y: f32,
    84:         terminal_w: f32,
    85:         terminal_h: f32,
    86:         ui_text: &[TextCommand],
    87:     ) {
    88:         let mut vertices = if let Some(grid) = grid {
    89:             atlas_vertex_builder::build_vertices(
    90:                 grid,
    91:                 &mut self.glyph_atlas,
    92:                 &self.font_metrics,
    93:                 &mut *self.rasterizer,
    94:                 self.default_fg,
    95:                 self.default_bg,
    96:                 full_width,
    97:                 full_height,
    98:                 offset_x,
    99:                 offset_y,
   100:                 terminal_w,
   101:                 terminal_h,
   102:             )
   103:         } else {
   104:             Vec::new()
   105:         };
   106: 
   107:         // Rasterize UI chrome text through the same atlas.
   108:         // UI text uses transparent bg (alpha=0) to trigger grayscale AA mode
   109:         // in the atlas shader, so text alpha-composites cleanly over gradient
   110:         // and SDF-rounded backgrounds drawn by the quad pipeline.
   111:         let phys = self.font_metrics.scaled_for_render();
   112:         let vw = full_width as f32;
   113:         let vh = full_height as f32;
   114:         let transparent_bg = [0.0f32, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0];
   115: 
   116:         for cmd in ui_text {
   117:             let font_size = phys.font_size * cmd.scale;
   118:             let use_ui_font = !matches!(cmd.font_kind, TextFontKind::TerminalMono);
   119:             let use_serif = matches!(cmd.font_kind, TextFontKind::UiSerif);
   120:             let mut fallback_x = cmd.x;
   121: 
   122:             for (index, ch) in cmd.text.chars().enumerate() {
   123:                 let entry = match cmd.font_kind {
   124:                     TextFontKind::TerminalMono => {
   125:                         let key = GlyphKey::new(ch, font_size, cmd.bold, cmd.italic);
   126:                         self.glyph_atlas
   127:                             .get_or_insert(key, &mut *self.rasterizer, font_size)
   128:                     }
   129:                     TextFontKind::UiSans => {
   130:                         let Some(rast) = self.ui_rasterizer.as_mut() else { continue };
   131:                         let key = GlyphKey::new_font(ch, font_size, cmd.bold, cmd.italic, 1);
   132:                         self.glyph_atlas.get_or_insert(key, &mut **rast, font_size)
   133:                     }
   134:                     TextFontKind::UiSerif => {
   135:                         let Some(rast) = self
   136:                             .ui_serif_rasterizer
   137:                             .as_mut()
   138:                             .or(self.ui_rasterizer.as_mut())
   139:                         else {
   140:                             continue;
   141:                         };
   142:                         let key = GlyphKey::new_font(ch, font_size, cmd.bold, cmd.italic, 2);
   143:                         self.glyph_atlas.get_or_insert(key, &mut **rast, font_size)
   144:                     }
   145:                 };
   146: 
   147:                 let px = cmd
   148:                     .glyph_offsets
   149:                     .get(index)
   150:                     .map_or(fallback_x, |offset| cmd.x + *offset);
   151:                 let py = cmd.y;
   152:                 let sc = cmd.scale;
   153:                 // For proportional font, use actual glyph slot width from UV;
   154:                 // for monospace, use cell_width.
   155:                 let pw = if use_ui_font {
   156:                     // Slot width = atlas slot pixel width from UV coords
   157:                     (entry.u1 - entry.u0) * self.glyph_atlas.atlas_width() as f32 * sc
   158:                 } else {
   159:                     phys.cell_width * sc
   160:                 };
   161:                 let ph = phys.cell_height * sc;
   162: 
   163:                 let x0 = px / vw * 2.0 - 1.0;
   164:                 let y0 = 1.0 - py / vh * 2.0;
   165:                 let x1 = (px + pw) / vw * 2.0 - 1.0;
   166:                 let y1 = 1.0 - (py + ph) / vh * 2.0;
   167: 
   168:                 // 6 vertices (2 triangles)
   169:                 let v = |pos: [f32; 2], uv: [f32; 2]| CellVertex {
   170:                     position: pos,
   171:                     uv,
   172:                     fg_color: cmd.fg,
   173:                     bg_color: match cmd.composite {
   174:                         TextCompositeMode::FlatBackground => [cmd.bg[0], cmd.bg[1], cmd.bg[2], 1.0],
   175:                         TextCompositeMode::MixedBackground => transparent_bg,
   176:                     },
   177:                 };
   178:                 let tl = [x0, y0];
   179:                 let tr = [x1, y0];
   180:                 let bl = [x0, y1];
   181:                 let br = [x1, y1];
   182:                 let uv_tl = [entry.u0, entry.v0];
   183:                 let uv_br = [entry.u1, entry.v1];
   184:                 let uv_tr = [uv_br[0], uv_tl[1]];
   185:                 let uv_bl = [uv_tl[0], uv_br[1]];
   186: 
   187:                 vertices.push(v(tl, uv_tl));
   188:                 vertices.push(v(tr, uv_tr));
   189:                 vertices.push(v(bl, uv_bl));
   190:                 vertices.push(v(tr, uv_tr));
   191:                 vertices.push(v(bl, uv_bl));
   192:                 vertices.push(v(br, uv_br));
   193: 
   194:                 if cmd.glyph_offsets.is_empty() {
   195:                     fallback_x += (if use_ui_font || use_serif {
   196:                         entry.advance
   197:                     } else {
   198:                         phys.cell_width
   199:                     }) * sc;
   200:                 }
   201:             }
   202:         }
   203: 
   204:         if vertices.is_empty() { return; }
   205: 
   206:         let atlas_update = self.glyph_atlas.take_dirty_data();
   207:         let program = AtlasShaderProgram {
   208:             vertices,
   209:             atlas_update,
   210:             viewport_size: (full_width, full_height),
   211:         };
   212:         let primitive = program.build_primitive();
   213:         primitive.prepare(&mut self.pipeline, device, queue);
   214:     }
   215: 
   216:     /// Draw the prepared terminal content into the render pass.
   217:     pub fn draw(&self, render_pass: &mut wgpu::RenderPass<'_>) {
   218:         // AtlasPipeline stores vertex_count from prepare; draw uses the stored pipeline state
   219:         // We need a dummy primitive to call draw - reuse the pipeline's stored vertex count
   220:         let primitive = godly_terminal_surface::atlas_shader::AtlasShaderProgram {
   221:             vertices: vec![],
   222:             atlas_update: None,
   223:             viewport_size: (0, 0),
   224:         }.build_primitive();
   225:         primitive.draw(&self.pipeline, render_pass);
   226:     }

   229:         &self.font_metrics
   230:     }
